I haven't really noticed it at home or anything. Just... well, just there, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When you're holding the spoon in the air or folding the napkins repeatedly, are you aware that you're doing it, or does it feel like you're not fully in control of those actions?\n\nPatient: I don't... I couldn't really say. I'm just... standing there. I don't think much on it until somebody says my name or taps my arm. I just... I don't know.\n\nDoctor: When you're holding the spoon or folding the napkins, do you realize you're doing it, or does it feel like you're watching yourself from the outside?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think it's like that. I ain't outside myself or nothing. I'm just... right there. I just ain't paying much mind to it, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: While you're doing these things, like holding the spoon or folding the napkins, does it feel like you're on autopilot or like you're not fully aware of what you're doing at the moment?\n\nPatient: I reckon it's... well, like I said, I'm just standing by the potluck table. I just... I hold the serving spoon up and don't move, or I'm folding that same stack of napkins over and over. Folks are waiting on me, but... I don't rightly know if it's autopilot. I guess I'm just there. I ain't asleep or nothing.
